Read the upstream diff range from Step 2 via\n45\t   `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/compare/<pinned>...<head>`\n46\t   (or `git log --oneline <pinned>..<head>` against an upstream clone).\n47\t2. Classify each upstream commit into one of:\n48\t   - **Security** — CVE fix, sandbox escape, command-injection patch.\n49\t   - **Behavior** — change to prompt detection, ANSI stripping, line buffering,\n50\t     timeout semantics, `pexpect.spawn` argument handling.\n51\t   - **Maintenance** — docs, CI, formatting, dependency floors, test-only changes.\n52\t3. Any **Security** classification escalates to a resync task in the same\n53\t   review pass. **Behavior** classifications require a judgment call from the\n54\t   review owner (resync if our consumers — `PtyDriver`, `PtyStream` — depend on\n55\t   the changed surface; defer otherwise with a recorded rationale).\n56\t   **Maintenance** classifications never trigger a resync.\n57\t\n58\t## Step 4 — Re-verify license + attribution alignment\n59\t\n60\t1. The review\nis a *read-only* re-verification of the pinned upstream SHA; resync work is\nspun out into its own task only when the checklist surfaces a security- or\nbehavior-relevant change. The default outcome of a clean review is to roll the\nreview anchor forward 90 days and record the row in `PROVENANCE.md` §4.\n\nA review may be triggered out-of-band (i.e. before the next scheduled date) by\nany of:\n\n- A CVE published against `pexpect` or `ptytest` upstream.\n- An upstream commit touching the prompt-detection or ANSI-stripping code paths.\n- A failing CI run on `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_vendor.py` after an unrelated\n  refactor (signal that the pin has implicitly diverged from on-disk content).\n\n## Step 1 — Confirm the current pin\n\n1. Open `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` and copy the **Pinned\n   upstream SHA** from §2.\n2. Confirm the SHA matches the upstream commit it claims by visiting\n   `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/commit/<pinned-sha>` and\n   verifying the commit page loads (i.e. upstream has not force-pushed the SHA\n   out of existence).\n3. If the SHA no longer resolves upstream, **escalate to a resync task\n   immediately** — a missing pin is a release blocker, not a quarterly review\n   item.\n\n## Step 2 — Fetch the current upstream HEAD\n\n1. Query `GET https://api.github.com/repos/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/commits/master`\n   (or equivalent) and record the current upstream HEAD SHA and commit date.\n2. If `upstream HEAD == pinned SHA`, jump to Step 5 (no drift; record clean\n   review).\n3. Otherwise, record the diff range `<pinned-sha>..<head-sha>` for Step 3.\n\n## Step 3 — Triage the upstream diff\n\n1. Read the upstream diff range from Step 2 via\n   `https://github.com/brandon-fryslie/ptytest/compare/<pinned>...<head>`\n   (or `git log --oneline <pinned>..<head>` against an upstream clone).\n2. Classify each upstream commit into one of:\n   - **Security** — CVE fix, sandbox escape, command-injection patch.\n   - **Behavior** — change to prompt detection, ANSI stripping, line buffering,\n     timeout semantics, `pexpect.spawn` argument handling.\n   - **Maintenance** — docs, CI, formatting, dependency floors, test-only changes.\n3. Any **Security** classification escalates to a resync task in the same\n   review pass. **Behavior** classifications require a judgment call from the\n   review owner (resync if our consumers — `PtyDriver`, `PtyStream` — depend on\n   the changed surface; defer otherwise with a recorded rationale).\n   **Maintenance** classifications never trigger a resync.\n\n## Step 4 — Re-verify license + attribution alignment\n\n1. Diff the upstream `LICENSE` file at the new HEAD against\n   `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE`. They MUST be byte-identical text\n   (whitespace included) for the pin to remain valid.\n2. If the upstream LICENSE text changed:\n   - Update `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` in the same commit as any\n     SHA resync.\n   - Re-verify the canonical attribution clause in the top-level `NOTICE`\n     against any upstream LICENSE text changes; update both `NOTICE` and the\n     D-10 ADR body in `.dev/releases/current/cliEval/decisions.md` if the\n     clause moved.\n3. If the upstream LICENSE text is unchanged but the pin is being held (no\n   resync), record this verification in the review log row anyway — it is the\n   AC10 commitment.\n\n## Step 5 — Record the review outcome\n\n1. Append a row to `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/PROVENANCE.md` §4 with:\n   - **Date** of review (`YYYY-MM-DD`).\n   - **Reviewer** name.\n   - **Upstream SHA at review** (the HEAD captured in Step 2).\n   - **Outcome** — one of: *Pin held* / *Resync scheduled* / *Resync landed*.\n   - **Notes** — 1-line summary of the triage call (e.g. *\"3 maintenance\n     commits upstream; no behavior drift; pin held.\"*).\n2. If outcome is *Pin held*: bump the **Next review due** date in §3 by 90 days\n   from today.\n3. If outcome is *Resync scheduled* or *Resync landed*: open / link a tracked\n   resync task, follow the §3 *Resync procedure* steps, and set **Next review\n   due** based on whether the resync is in the same window (keep anchor) or\n   carries past (bump 90 days from the resync landing date).\n\n---\n\n**Acceptance:** This checklist satisfies AC10 (fork SHA pin + drift policy). It\nis intentionally a *procedure* document, not an automation. Automation of the\nquarterly trigger (e.g. a calendar reminder or a CI job that opens an issue\n90 days after the last review row) is out of scope for T02.03 and may be added\nlater under a separate R5-mitigation task (per `T02.26` notes in\n`phase-2-tasklist.md`).\n","numLines":98,"startLine":1,"totalLines":98}}}

See §OPS-001 Closure below; per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0021/spec.md`.\n- R4 (2026-05-20): DOC-OQ4 closure — D-10 added recording the NOTICE/LICENSE attribution mechanism for the vendored ptytest fork. OQ-4 status flips from OPEN to RESOLVED (M2 entry blocker for T02.01 cleared); per-deliverable spec at `artifacts/D-0024/spec.md`.\n\n---\n\n## D-1: PTY layer — fork ptytest vs build minimal pexpect wrapper\n\n### Context\n\nReal Claude Code is a TTY-based interactive REPL. The eval harness needs a PTY (pseudo-terminal) driver that can spawn it, write to its stdin, read from its stdout/stderr, detect \"prompt ready\" states, kill on timeout, and capture exit codes.\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons | LOC | Risk |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| **A. Fork `brandon-fryslie/ptytest`** | Working Claude Code example already exists in repo. Plugin-free standalone class. Active enough (recent commits). | 1 star / 0 forks → bus-factor real. Includes pytest-fixture machinery we don't need. | ~300 LOC adaptation | LOW (ptytest is small, easy to own) |\n| B. Build minimal `pexpect` wrapper from scratch | Clean ownership, no upstream-drift risk, exactly the API we want. | More code to write, debug, test. We re-derive Claude Code TTY quirks. | ~700 LOC fresh | MEDIUM (pexpect has subtle gotchas) |\n| C. Use `pexpect` directly (no wrapper) | Zero abstraction overhead. | Every eval re-implements spawn/wait/expect_prompt patterns; code duplication. | ~0 LOC harness; +~50 LOC per eval | HIGH (drift across evals) |\n\n### Decision: **A — fork ptytest, vendor under `cli/eval/pty/`, fork-and-own posture.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The fork-research report verified ptytest has a working Claude Code example, which means the hardest TTY edge cases (prompt detection, ANSI escape handling, line-buffered reads) are already solved.\n2. Bus-factor risk is mitigated by **vendoring** (we copy the code in; no runtime pip dependency on the upstream repo). If upstream dies, we have a frozen working copy.\n3. The durable dependency is `pexpect` (2.8k stars, active again at v4.9) — ptytest is just a thin layer on top.\n4. 300 LOC adaptation is ~5x cheaper than 700 LOC fresh build.\n5. Option C (raw pexpect) fails the harness-cleanliness goal — evals should look like assertion code, not PTY plumbing.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- We own a small vendored fork forever. `PROVENANCE.md` documents the fork SHA and our diffs.\n- Quarterly review of upstream `pexpect` releases; resync if security-relevant.\n- A new file at `cli/eval/pty/LICENSE` reproducing upstream MIT terms (license compliance).\n\n---\n\n## D-2: Assertion DSL — adopt `Expect.*` port vs raw assertions\n\n### Context\n\nEach eval needs to verify hook side-effects (file presence, JSONL events, settings.json registrations, exit codes, TTY output). The expression of these assertions can be:\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Port `mcp-eval`'s `Expect.tools.*` DSL idea as Python primitives** | Declarative YAML manifest possible. Reusable across evals. Single place to add new assertion types. Mental-model match with industry pattern. | +~200 LOC for the DSL itself. Some abstraction overhead (declarative → callable). |\n| B. Raw Python `assert` statements in each eval | Lowest abstraction. No new code. | Each eval is more code. Declarative YAML manifest not possible (or only partial). Failure messages are ad-hoc per eval. |\n| C. Use pytest's assertion machinery directly | Free; idiomatic. | Couples harness to pytest, but the harness is NOT pytest-driven (it's a CLI subcommand). |\n\n### Decision: **A — port `Expect.*` DSL, ~200 LOC, no upstream dependency.**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. The YAML manifest schema (§5 of design-spec) is a hard requirement to keep eval definitions declarative and reviewable. Raw Python assertions can't be expressed in YAML.\n2. A small DSL (~10 method types: `file.exists`, `file.absent`, `jsonl.contains_event`, `jsonl.event_count`, `settings_json.has_registration`, `exit_code.equals`, `stderr.contains`, `stdout.contains`, `duration.less_than`, `duration.greater_than`) covers all 15 evals comfortably.\n3. The mcp-eval API surface is a proven mental model — we steal the ergonomics without taking a dependency on the framework (which has the wrong architecture for our needs).\n4. Failure messages from a typed DSL are uniformly high-quality (the DSL knows its own structure); ad-hoc assertions tend to produce noisy error output.\n5. The DSL has a programmatic escape hatch for parameterized evals that can't be expressed in YAML — so we don't lock ourselves out of complex assertions.\n\n### Consequences\n\n- `cli/eval/expect.py` is a public API surface; changes need backward-compat care once evals are written against it.\n- The YAML schema (`suites/suite.schema.json`) and the DSL must stay in lockstep — `loader.py` translates declarative → callable.\n- Out-of-scope for v1: LLM-judge assertions (mcp-eval has these; we don't need them for hook validation).\n\n---\n\n## D-3: HOME isolation — extend existing `IsolationLayers` vs separate class\n\n### Context\n\n`src/superclaude/cli/sprint/executor.py:107-182` already implements 4-layer per-subprocess isolation (`CLAUDE_WORK_DIR`, `GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES`, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DIR`, `CLAUDE_SETTINGS_DIR`). The eval harness needs a 5th layer: `HOME` override (and `XDG_*` companions). Question: extend the existing class or build a new one?\n\n### Options\n\n| Option | Pros | Cons |\n|---|---|---|\n| **A. Compose `IsolationLayers` in a new `HomeIsolation` class** | Reuses 4 existing layers for free. No risk to sprint's existing usage. New `HOME` layer lives in eval code, not sprint code. | Two classes to maintain. Slight indirection. |\n| B. Add `HOME` layer to `IsolationLayers` directly (modify sprint code) | One unified class. Sprint also benefits from HOME isolation (theoretical future use). | Modifies code outside this release's scope. Sprint doesn't currently need HOME isolation, so YAGNI risk. Sprint regression risk. |\n| C. Build `HomeIsolation` standalone (no inheritance/composition) | Maximum decoupling. | Duplicate 4 layers of code. Bug-fixes diverge over time. |\n\n### Decision: **A — `HomeIsolation` COMPOSES `IsolationLayers` (has-a, not is-a).**\n\n### Rationale\n\n1. Composition preserves sprint's existing class verbatim. Zero risk to PR #49-era infrastructure.\n2. The `HOME` layer is conceptually a \"5th layer,\" but its lifecycle (mkdtemp → install hooks → seed state → teardown) is more complex than the existing 4 (which are just env-var settings). Keeping it separate cleanly captures that asymmetry.\n3. If a future sprint feature ever needs HOME isolation, it can either re-use `HomeIsolation` directly or migrate the logic upward — easier path than splitting later.\n4. The interface in `design-spec.md` §7 already reflects this: `HomeIsolation` exposes `.env()` that returns the dict overlay, which the caller merges with `IsolationLayers.env()` output.","is_error":false}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"772ae136-e46c-4038-aba7-d58b744d5b50","uuid":"20fc1f84-19e8-440d-b067-eec8897ab52e","timestamp":"2026-05-20T09:15:11.061Z","tool_use_result":{"stdout":"# cliEval — Architectural Decisions Log\n\n**Status:** 🟡 PROPOSED-R2 2026-05-18 (pending maintainer sign-off)\n**Format:** ADR-lite (one decision per section: Context → Options → Decision → Consequences)\n\n**Revision log:**\n- R1 (2026-05-18): D-1..D-4 initial proposals.\n- R2 (2026-05-18): D-5..D-8 added to resolve the 4 CRITICAL findings from `spec-panel-review.md`.\n- R3 (2026-05-20): OPS-001 closure — D-5..D-8 status flipped to \"queued for sign-off\"; OQ-1/3/7/8/10 resolution-status block added; implementation gates cross-referenced to ADR IDs. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TxqXHjb6zSSbQAfwWBjsUd","type":"tool_result","content":"740\t\n741\t- No fancy async/await. Each eval is a blocking thread; the orchestrator just collects results via `as_completed`.\n742\t- No process supervisor / reaper daemon. Python's `concurrent.futures` cleans up worker threads on shutdown.\n743\t- No distributed mode. Single-host only.\n744\t\n745\t---\n746\t\n747\t## 13. Fork strategy — vendored `ptytest`\n748\t\n749\t### What we fork\n750\t\n751\t`brandon-fryslie/ptytest` (MIT, Python 3.8+). The repo has 1 star / 0 forks — bus-factor risk is real. Mitigation: **fork-and-own**.\n752\t\n753\t### Vendoring layout\n754\t\n755\t```\n756\tsrc/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/\n757\t├── __init__.py\n758\t├── driver.py        # PtyDriver (was: ptytest/__init__.py PtySession)\n759\t├── stream.py        # ANSI strip + line buffer (was: ptytest/_stream.py)\n760\t├── LICENSE          # upstream MIT, verbatim\n761\t└── PROVENANCE.md    # fork SHA, what we changed, why\n762\t```\n763\t\n764\t### What we change vs upstream\n765\t\n766\t| Change | Reason |\n767\t|---|---|\n768\t| Rename `PtySession` → `PtyDriver` | Avoid pytest-fixture connotation; we use it in a non-pytest context too. |\n769\t| Remove pytest-plugin entry-point | We don't need fixture autoloading. |\n770\t| Add `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=)` method | Cleaner waiting for Claude Code's TTY-prompt signal (the `>` or `$` indicator that input is welcome). |\n771\t| Add `inject_prompt(text)` method | Adds CR-LF + flushes — wraps stdin writes for Claude Code idioms. |\n772\t| Tighten dependency to `pexpect>=4.9` | The vendored ptytest doesn't pin pexpect tightly. |\n773\t| Apply ANSI-strip aggressively | Claude Code emits rich ANSI; the eval's TTY assertions need plain text. |\n774\t\n775\t### What we DON'T change\n776\t\n777\t- Core `pexpect.spawn` mechanics — leave it alone.\n778\t- The Docker-isolation mode (we don't need it; per-eval HOME isolation is enough).\n779\t- The original test suite — we vendor it under `tests/cli/test_eval/test_pty_vendor.py` to catch regressions during future ptytest re-syncs.\n780\t\n781\t### Upstream resync policy\n782\t\n783\tThe vendored fork is \"frozen at the fork SHA.\" If upstream releases significant changes (e.g., `pexpect` major-version bump, important bugfix), the `PROVENANCE.md` documents the resync procedure: pull upstream, three-way merge our changes, re-run vendored tests.\n784\t\n785\t### License compliance\n786\t\n787\tptytest is MIT. Pin a\nsingle integration site at\n`src/superclaude/cli/eval/hook_adapter.py` so the cliEval harness and\nthe production user-install path stay byte-for-byte aligned.\n\n## Adapter contract\n\n### Signature\n\n```python\ndef deploy_hooks_to(home_path: Path) -> None: ...\n```\n\n`home_path` is the per-eval HOME directory `HomeIsolation.setup`\nmaterialized (i.e., `<scratch_root>/<eval_id>-XXXXXX/`). The adapter\nreturns `None` on success and raises `HookDeployFailed` on any failure.\n\n### Side effects (in order)\n\n1. **Defense-in-depth refusal.** Resolve both `home_path` and\n   `home_path / .claude` with `strict=False` and reject if either\n   intersects the real `~/.claude/` directory (equality OR descendant).\n   This catches: (a) `home_path` symlinked to real HOME, (b) callers\n   passing their `$HOME` as `home_path` (which would direct the\n   `<home>/.claude/settings.json` write straight into real `~/.claude/`).\n2. **`install_hooks` delegation.** Invoke\n   `install_hooks(target_path=home_path / \".claude\" / \"settings.json\",\n   force=False)`. The installer copies hook scripts to\n   `<home>/.claude/hooks/` and merges hook registrations into\n   `<home>/.claude/settings.json`.\n3. **Verbatim `hooks.json` copy.** Copy\n   `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` byte-for-byte to\n   `<home>/.claude/hooks.json` via the dedicated\n   `_copy_hooks_json_verbatim` helper (a thin wrapper over\n   `shutil.copy2` whose only purpose is to be a clean monkeypatch seam\n   for tests).\n\n### Idempotency\n\nRe-invocation on the same `home_path` produces identical filesystem\nstate. `install_hooks` is itself idempotent (existing scripts are\nskipped without `--force`; the settings.json merge skips matcher\ncollisions). The verbatim copy uses `shutil.copy2` which overwrites\nwith identical bytes when the source is unchanged.\n\n### Error contract — `HookDeployFailed.error_tag`\n\nThe exception carries a structured `error_tag` so `EvalRunner`\n(T03.x) can route the failure into `EvalRunner.outcome.artifacts`\nwithout parsing human-readable strings.\n\n| `error_tag`                  | Triggered when                                                                 |\n|------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `refused-real-home`          | `home_path` or `home_path/.claude` resolves to (or under) real `~/.claude/`.   |\n| `source-hooks-json-missing`  | `src/superclaude/hooks/hooks.json` is absent on disk (broken checkout).        |\n| `install-hooks-failed`       | `install_hooks` returned `success=False`. Installer message in `detail`.       |\n| `hooks-json-copy-failed`     | `_copy_hooks_json_verbatim` raised `OSError`. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01AqaVmjMcLdQJFdoduRT1Vq","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_019ggBxVe2SCwFCnSh6MbVkG","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty_driver.py","content":"\"\"\"COMP-007 PtyDriver -- pexpect.spawn wrapper for real Claude Code subprocess.\n\nThis module satisfies FR-G1 (real-subprocess discipline) by spawning the real\n``claude`` binary via a pseudo-terminal. It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n\nThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\nimports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\nlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\nvendored sources are still landing. Once T02.01 lands its sources, the\nfall-back path becomes dead code -- this is intentional, not a long-term\nescape hatch.\n\nTest stubs:\n\nThe unit tests in ``tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`` spawn a\nself-contained Python stub (a small script that prints a prompt-ready\nindicator, echoes a single line, and exits with a known code) rather than\nthe real ``claude`` binary. This keeps the unit tests deterministic and\nhost-portable while still exercising the full PTY round-trip.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Pattern\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n    import pexpect as _pexpect_module\n\ntry:  # Prefer the vendored fork once T02.01 lands.\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\nexcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - exercised until T02.01 lands sources\n    import pexpect as _pexpect\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN\",\n    \"PtyDriver\",\n    \"PtyDriverError\",\n    \"PtyDriverTimeout\",\n    \"PtyDriverNotStarted\",\n    \"PtyDriverEOF\",\n]\n\n\n# Default prompt-ready pattern. Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits at\n# the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: the bare ``>`` and the bare ``$``. The\n# pattern is intentionally lenient (trailing whitespace allowed) because\n# pexpect's buffer may include the trailing carriage return between frames.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"(?:\\r?\\n|^)[>$] *$\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriver:\n    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n\n    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n    prompt_ready_pattern:\n        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN`.\n    default_timeout:\n        Wall-clock seconds used by ``expect_prompt_ready`` and ``wait_exit``\n        when the caller does not specify a per-call timeout.\n    encoding:\n        Text encoding used by ``pexpect.spawn``. ``\"utf-8\"`` is the default;\n        pass ``None`` to receive raw ``bytes`` (rarely useful for Claude Code,\n        whose TTY output is text-mode UTF-8).\n    dimensions:\n        ``(rows, cols)`` of the simulated terminal. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":252,"cache_read_input_tokens":124796,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":252,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":74,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"772ae136-e46c-4038-aba7-d58b744d5b50","uuid":"c213a8e5-8d25-4807-8959-3b56f8f10bd6"}

It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n\nThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\nimports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\nlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\nvendored sources are still landing. The\n# pattern is intentionally lenient (trailing whitespace allowed) because\n# pexpect's buffer may include the trailing carriage return between frames.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"(?:\\r?\\n|^)[>$] *$\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriver:\n    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n\n    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n    prompt_ready_pattern:\n        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN`.\n    default_timeout:\n        Wall-clock seconds used by ``expect_prompt_ready`` and ``wait_exit``\n        when the caller does not specify a per-call timeout.\n    encoding:\n        Text encoding used by ``pexpect.spawn``. ``\"utf-8\"`` is the default;\n        pass ``None`` to receive raw ``bytes`` (rarely useful for Claude Code,\n        whose TTY output is text-mode UTF-8).\n    dimensions:\n        ``(rows, cols)`` of the simulated terminal. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits\n# at the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: a bare ``>`` or ``$`` followed by\n# optional whitespace and a CR/LF terminator. The pattern relies on an\n# explicit newline rather than the ``$`` end-of-string anchor because\n# ``pexpect`` compiles patterns without MULTILINE and the incremental PTY\n# buffer almost always has trailing CR/LF after the marker -- an unanchored\n# match against the newline is both more permissive and more reliable.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r?\\n\"","originalFile":"\"\"\"COMP-007 PtyDriver -- pexpect.spawn wrapper for real Claude Code subprocess.\n\nThis module satisfies FR-G1 (real-subprocess discipline) by spawning the real\n``claude`` binary via a pseudo-terminal. It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n\nThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\nimports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\nlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\nvendored sources are still landing. Once T02.01 lands its sources, the\nfall-back path becomes dead code -- this is intentional, not a long-term\nescape hatch.\n\nTest stubs:\n\nThe unit tests in ``tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`` spawn a\nself-contained Python stub (a small script that prints a prompt-ready\nindicator, echoes a single line, and exits with a known code) rather than\nthe real ``claude`` binary. This keeps the unit tests deterministic and\nhost-portable while still exercising the full PTY round-trip.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Pattern\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n    import pexpect as _pexpect_module\n\ntry:  # Prefer the vendored fork once T02.01 lands.\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\nexcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - exercised until T02.01 lands sources\n    import pexpect as _pexpect\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN\",\n    \"PtyDriver\",\n    \"PtyDriverError\",\n    \"PtyDriverTimeout\",\n    \"PtyDriverNotStarted\",\n    \"PtyDriverEOF\",\n]\n\n\n# Default prompt-ready pattern. Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits at\n# the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: the bare ``>`` and the bare ``$``. The\n# pattern is intentionally lenient (trailing whitespace allowed) because\n# pexpect's buffer may include the trailing carriage return between frames.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"(?:\\r?\\n|^)[>$] *$\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriver:\n    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n\n    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n    prompt_ready_pattern:\n        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN`.\n    default_timeout:\n        Wall-clock seconds used by ``expect_prompt_ready`` and ``wait_exit``\n        when the caller does not specify a per-call timeout.\n    encoding:\n        Text encoding used by ``pexpect.spawn``. ``\"utf-8\"`` is the default;\n        pass ``None`` to receive raw ``bytes`` (rarely useful for Claude Code,\n        whose TTY output is text-mode UTF-8).\n    dimensions:\n        ``(rows, cols)`` of the simulated terminal. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":64,"oldLines":11,"newStart":64,"newLines":14,"lines":[" ]"," "," ","-# Default prompt-ready pattern.
