It deliberately avoids any in-process\nSDK shortcut: every interaction with the child goes through the PTY's stdin /\nstdout file descriptors, exactly as a human user would drive the REPL.\n\nThe class exposes the five method names enumerated in the COMP-007 roadmap row\n(R-036) and the T02.16 acceptance criteria:\n\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.expect_prompt_ready` -- block until the child emits a\n  \"prompt is ready for input\" indicator (``>`` or ``$`` on its own line by\n  default; configurable via ``prompt_ready_pattern``).\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.inject_prompt` -- write a user-style prompt to the child's\n  stdin, terminated by a carriage return + line-feed, and flush.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.write_stdin` -- raw write to the child's stdin without any\n  newline handling. Used for control sequences, partial-line input, or\n  test-stub probing.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.read_stdout` -- non-blocking read of the next chunk of bytes\n  from the PTY. Returns ``b\"\"`` immediately when no data is available.\n* :meth:`PtyDriver.wait_exit` -- wait for the child to exit and return its\n  exit code. Captures signal-termination as a negative exit code (POSIX\n  convention) so callers can distinguish ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM``.\n\nThe vendored ptytest fork (T02.01) lives at\n``src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty/`` and pins ``pexpect>=4.9`` via the vendored\nimports. This module first attempts to source ``pexpect`` from the vendored\nlocation and falls back to the top-level ``pexpect`` package while the\nvendored sources are still landing. Once T02.01 lands its sources, the\nfall-back path becomes dead code -- this is intentional, not a long-term\nescape hatch.\n\nTest stubs:\n\nThe unit tests in ``tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`` spawn a\nself-contained Python stub (a small script that prints a prompt-ready\nindicator, echoes a single line, and exits with a known code) rather than\nthe real ``claude`` binary. This keeps the unit tests deterministic and\nhost-portable while still exercising the full PTY round-trip.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any, Pattern\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:  # pragma: no cover - typing only\n    import pexpect as _pexpect_module\n\ntry:  # Prefer the vendored fork once T02.01 lands.\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # type: ignore[attr-defined]\nexcept ImportError:  # pragma: no cover - exercised until T02.01 lands sources\n    import pexpect as _pexpect\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN\",\n    \"PtyDriver\",\n    \"PtyDriverError\",\n    \"PtyDriverTimeout\",\n    \"PtyDriverNotStarted\",\n    \"PtyDriverEOF\",\n]\n\n\n# Default prompt-ready pattern. Matches the two indicators Claude Code emits\n# at the end of a \"ready for input\" frame: a bare ``>`` or ``$`` followed by\n# optional whitespace and a CR/LF terminator. The pattern relies on an\n# explicit newline rather than the ``$`` end-of-string anchor because\n# ``pexpect`` compiles patterns without MULTILINE and the incremental PTY\n# buffer almost always has trailing CR/LF after the marker -- an unanchored\n# match against the newline is both more permissive and more reliable.\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r?\\n\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverError(Exception):\n    \"\"\"Base class for ``PtyDriver`` failures.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverTimeout(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an ``expect`` or ``wait`` exceeded its timeout budget.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverNotStarted(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when an interaction method is called before ``spawn`` completed.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriverEOF(PtyDriverError):\n    \"\"\"Raised when the child closed its PTY before the expected event.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass PtyDriver:\n    \"\"\"Wraps ``pexpect.spawn`` for the IronClaude real-eval harness.\n\n    The constructor does **not** spawn the child; it only validates arguments\n    and stores them. Call :meth:`spawn` (or use the class as a context manager)\n    to actually launch the subprocess. This split keeps the constructor cheap\n    and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many drivers, spawn them in\n    parallel\" pattern.\n\n    Parameters\n    ----------\n    command:\n        Either a single executable string or a ``[command, *args]`` list.\n        Mirrors the ``pexpect.spawn`` first-argument contract.\n    env:\n        Optional environment overlay. ``None`` inherits the parent process\n        environment. To run with the per-eval HOME, pass\n        ``HomeIsolation.env()`` here.\n    cwd:\n        Working directory for the child. ``None`` inherits the parent CWD.\n    prompt_ready_pattern:\n        Regex matched against the PTY output by\n        :meth:`expect_prompt_ready`. Defaults to\n        :data:`DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN`.\n    default_timeout:\n        Wall-clock seconds used by ``expect_prompt_ready`` and ``wait_exit``\n        when the caller does not specify a per-call timeout.\n    encoding:\n        Text encoding used by ``pexpect.spawn``. ``\"utf-8\"`` is the default;\n        pass ``None`` to receive raw ``bytes`` (rarely useful for Claude Code,\n        whose TTY output is text-mode UTF-8).\n    dimensions:\n        ``(rows, cols)`` of the simulated terminal. The default\n        ``(40, 120)`` matches Claude Code's recommended dev-host geometry.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        command: str | list[str],\n        *,\n        env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n        cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n        prompt_ready_pattern: str | Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n        default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n        encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n        dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n    ) -> None:\n        if isinstance(command, str):\n            self._argv: list[str] = [command]\n        else:\n            if not command:\n                raise ValueError(\"command must be a non-empty string or list\")\n            self._argv = list(command)\n\n        self._env: dict[str, str] | None = dict(env) if env is not None else None\n        self._cwd: str | None = str(cwd) if cwd is not None else None\n\n        if isinstance(prompt_ready_pattern, str):\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern: Pattern[str] = re.compile(prompt_ready_pattern)\n        else:\n            self._prompt_ready_pattern = prompt_ready_pattern\n\n        if default_timeout <= 0:\n            raise ValueError(\"default_timeout must be > 0\")\n        self._default_timeout = float(default_timeout)\n        self._encoding = encoding\n        self._dimensions = dimensions\n\n        self._child: Any | None = None  # pexpect.spawn instance once spawned\n        self._exit_code: int | None = None\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ lifecycle\n\n    def spawn(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Launch the child subprocess via ``pexpect.spawn``.\n\n        Idempotent: a second call after the child has exited starts a fresh\n        process; a second call while the child is still alive raises\n        :class:`PtyDriverError`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is not None and self.is_alive():\n            raise PtyDriverError(\"PtyDriver.spawn called while child is still alive\")\n\n        rows, cols = self._dimensions\n        argv0, *args = self._argv\n        self._child = _pexpect.spawn(\n            argv0,\n            args=args,\n            env=self._env,\n            cwd=self._cwd,\n            timeout=self._default_timeout,\n            encoding=self._encoding,\n            dimensions=(rows, cols),\n        )\n        self._exit_code = None\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"PtyDriver\":\n        self.spawn()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        try:\n            if self.is_alive():\n                self.terminate()\n        finally:\n            self.close()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ state\n\n    def is_alive(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` while the child is running.\"\"\"\n\n        return self._child is not None and bool(self._child.isalive())\n\n    @property\n    def pid(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"PID of the child process, or ``None`` if not yet spawned.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return None\n        return int(self._child.pid)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ I/O\n\n    def _require_child(self) -> Any:\n        if self._child is None:\n            raise PtyDriverNotStarted(\"spawn() must be called before interaction\")\n        return self._child\n\n    def expect_prompt_ready(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Block until the child emits the prompt-ready indicator.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's\n            ``default_timeout``. Pass an explicit value to override on a\n            per-call basis.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        str\n            The bytes captured *before* the prompt-ready match. Useful when\n            the caller wants to inspect banner / version output the child\n            emitted prior to becoming interactive.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The prompt-ready pattern did not appear within ``timeout``\n            seconds.\n        PtyDriverEOF\n            The child closed its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting\n            the prompt-ready pattern.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            child.expect(self._prompt_ready_pattern, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"prompt_ready_pattern not seen within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n        except _pexpect.EOF as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverEOF(\n                \"child closed PTY before prompt-ready pattern matched\"\n            ) from exc\n        before = child.before\n        return before if isinstance(before, str) else (before or b\"\").decode(\n            self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\"\n        )\n\n    def inject_prompt(self, text: str) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send a user-style prompt to the child, terminated by ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\n\n        Wraps ``pexpect.spawn.send`` with the carriage-return + line-feed\n        sequence Claude Code expects from a real TTY. The send is flushed\n        before returning.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if not isinstance(text, str):\n            raise TypeError(\"inject_prompt requires str (use write_stdin for bytes)\")\n        child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")\n        # pexpect.spawn writes via os.write which is unbuffered, but be explicit\n        # in case a subclass or vendored fork swaps the I/O layer.\n        flush = getattr(child, \"flush\", None)\n        if callable(flush):\n            try:\n                flush()\n            except (OSError, ValueError):\n                pass\n\n    def write_stdin(self, data: str | bytes) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Raw write to the child's stdin.\n\n        Returns the number of bytes (or characters when ``encoding`` is set)\n        that pexpect reports it wrote. No newline is appended; callers that\n        want a prompt-style write should use :meth:`inject_prompt`.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if isinstance(data, bytes) and self._encoding is not None:\n            data = data.decode(self._encoding, errors=\"strict\")\n        result = child.send(data)\n        return int(result) if result is not None else len(data)\n\n    def read_stdout(self, size: int = -1, timeout: float | None = None) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Non-blocking read of up to ``size`` bytes from the child PTY.\n\n        ``size`` is the maximum chunk size; negative values map to \"as much\n        as the PTY currently holds, capped at 65536 bytes.\" Returns an empty\n        string when no data is currently buffered (instead of blocking).\n\n        ``timeout`` is the maximum time pexpect will wait for the first byte.\n        ``None`` uses the driver's ``default_timeout``; pass ``0`` for a true\n        non-blocking poll.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        cap = 65536 if size is None or size < 0 else int(size)\n        if cap <= 0:\n            return \"\"\n        wait = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        try:\n            chunk = child.read_nonblocking(size=cap, timeout=wait)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT:\n            return \"\"\n        except _pexpect.EOF:\n            return \"\"\n        if isinstance(chunk, bytes):\n            chunk = chunk.decode(self._encoding or \"utf-8\", errors=\"replace\")\n        return chunk\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ exit\n\n    def wait_exit(self, timeout: float | None = None) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Wait for the child to exit and return its exit code.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        timeout:\n            Wall-clock seconds. ``None`` uses the driver's default timeout.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        int\n            POSIX exit code. Signal-terminated children return a negative\n            value (``-SIGTERM`` = ``-15``, etc.) so callers can distinguish\n            ``exit(124)`` from ``SIGTERM`` cleanly.\n\n        Raises\n        ------\n        PtyDriverTimeout\n            The child was still alive after ``timeout`` seconds. The child\n            remains running; call :meth:`terminate` to clean it up.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        child = self._require_child()\n        if self._exit_code is not None and not child.isalive():\n            return self._exit_code\n\n        effective_timeout = self._default_timeout if timeout is None else float(timeout)\n        # pexpect.wait() doesn't accept a timeout; emulate one by polling\n        # ``expect(EOF, timeout=...)`` which blocks until the PTY closes.\n        try:\n            child.expect(_pexpect.EOF, timeout=effective_timeout)\n        except _pexpect.TIMEOUT as exc:\n            raise PtyDriverTimeout(\n                f\"child did not exit within {effective_timeout:.2f}s\"\n            ) from exc\n\n        # Wait reaps the zombie and populates exitstatus / signalstatus.\n        try:\n            child.wait()\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # ``wait()`` raises if pexpect believes the child is still alive;\n            # in our flow EOF already arrived, so any residual race is\n            # benign -- isalive() will return False and exitstatus / signalstatus\n            # will be set on the next attribute read.\n            pass\n\n        if child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(child.signalstatus)\n        else:\n            self._exit_code = int(child.exitstatus) if child.exitstatus is not None else -1\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ cleanup\n\n    def terminate(self, force: bool = False) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Send SIGTERM (or SIGKILL when ``force=True``) to the child.\n\n        Safe to call when the child has already exited.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None or not self._child.isalive():\n            return\n        self._child.terminate(force=force)\n\n    def close(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Close the PTY file descriptors and release pexpect's bookkeeping.\"\"\"\n\n        if self._child is None:\n            return\n        try:\n            self._child.close(force=False)\n        except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n            # close() can raise when the child is still alive; force-close to\n            # avoid leaking the PTY fd. terminate() is safe to call twice.\n            try:\n                self._child.close(force=True)\n            except _pexpect.ExceptionPexpect:\n                pass\n        if self._child.signalstatus is not None:\n            self._exit_code = -int(self._child.signalstatus)\n        elif self._child.exitstatus is not None and self._exit_code is None:\n            self._exit_code = int(self._child.exitstatus)\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ helpers\n\n    @property\n    def exit_code(self) -> int | None:\n        \"\"\"Last-captured exit code (``None`` until the child has exited).\"\"\"\n\n        return self._exit_code\n\n    def fileno(self) -> int:\n        \"\"\"Return the PTY's read file descriptor for ``select``-based loops.\"\"\"\n\n        return int(self._require_child().fileno())\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":71,"oldLines":7,"newStart":71,"newLines":7,"lines":[" # ``pexpect`` compiles patterns without MULTILINE and the incremental PTY"," # buffer almost always has trailing CR/LF after the marker -- an unanchored"," # match against the newline is both more permissive and more reliable.","-DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r?\\n\"","+DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\""," "," "," class PtyDriverError(Exception):"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false}}

\"\"\"Python source that exits immediately without ever emitting the\n    prompt-ready marker. it must not add ``\\\\r\\\\n``.\"\"\"\n\n    source = textwrap.dedent(\n        \"\"\"\n        import sys\n        sys.stdout.write(\"> \\\\r\\\\n\"); sys.stdout.flush()\n        buf = sys.stdin.read(5)\n        sys.stdout.write(\"got=\" + repr(buf) + \"\\\\r\\\\n\")\n        sys.stdout.flush()\n        \"\"\"\n    ).strip()\n    with _make_driver(source) as driver:\n        driver.expect_prompt_ready()\n        wrote = driver.write_stdin(\"abcde\")\n        assert wrote >= 5\n        collected = \"\"\n        deadline = time.perf_counter() + 5.0\n        while time.perf_counter() < deadline:\n            collected += driver.read_stdout(timeout=0.5)\n            if \"got='abcde'\" in collected:\n                break\n        assert \"got='abcde'\" in collected\n\n\ndef test_read_stdout_returns_empty_string_when_idle() -> None:\n    \"\"\"Polling for output when none is available must not block forever.\"\"\"\n\n    with _make_driver(_prompt_stub_source()) as driver:\n        driver.expect_prompt_ready()\n        # The stub is now blocked on stdin.readline(); {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01DqxGExepSSZmALospqryMD","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01Wb8W6Uk5aiWmgRCNaQtsqa","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0036/spec.md","content":"# D-0036 — COMP-007 PtyDriver wrapping pexpect.spawn (Task T02.16)\n\n**Task**: T02.16 (Phase 2 — cliEval harness)\n**Tier**: STANDARD\n**Risk**: Medium\n**Roadmap**: R-036 / COMP-007 (PtyDriver)\n**Cross-links**: D-0023 (T02.01 vendored ptytest fork — runtime source of\n`pexpect>=4.9`), D-0034 (T02.14 hook adapter — sibling of the per-eval HOME\nflow), D-0035 (T02.15 HOME setup perf baseline — concurrency cap the driver\nruns under), D-0037 (T02.17 PtyStream — direct downstream consumer of\n`PtyDriver.read_stdout`).\n\n## Goal\n\nProvide a thin, well-tested wrapper around `pexpect.spawn` so the cliEval\nharness can drive the real `claude` binary through a pseudo-terminal exactly\nthe way a human user does — no in-process SDK shortcut, no subprocess\nwithout a TTY. This is the FR-G1 \"real subprocess discipline\" load-bearing\ncomponent: every eval body that needs to interact with `claude` does so\nthrough this class, and every `claude --help`-style smoke check the harness\nemits during `eval doctor` flows through the same code path.\n\n## Driver contract\n\n### Constructor\n\n```python\nPtyDriver(\n    command: str | list[str],\n    *,\n    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n    cwd: Path | str | None = None,\n    prompt_ready_pattern: str | re.Pattern[str] = DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN,\n    default_timeout: float = 30.0,\n    encoding: str | None = \"utf-8\",\n    dimensions: tuple[int, int] = (40, 120),\n) -> None\n```\n\nThe constructor validates arguments and stores them. It does **not** spawn\nthe child — that happens on `spawn()` or `__enter__`. This split keeps\nconstruction cheap and matches the cliEval orchestrator's \"build many\ndrivers, spawn them in parallel\" pattern (T03.16).\n\n### COMP-007 method set (T02.16 AC bullet 1)\n\n| Method                                 | Behavior                                                                 |\n|----------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `expect_prompt_ready(timeout=None)`    | Block until the prompt-ready pattern matches; return PTY buffer captured *before* the match. |\n| `inject_prompt(text: str)`             | `child.send(text + \"\\r\\n\")` then flush — Claude-Code-idiomatic write.    |\n| `write_stdin(data: str \\| bytes) -> int` | Raw write to the child's stdin; **no** newline appended.                  |\n| `read_stdout(size=-1, timeout=None)`   | Non-blocking read of up to `size` bytes from the PTY; returns `\"\"` when idle. |\n| `wait_exit(timeout=None) -> int`       | Wait for the child to exit; return its exit code.                        |\n\nTwo error classes carry timeout / EOF signals up to the caller:\n\n* `PtyDriverTimeout` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` and `wait_exit` when\n  the timeout budget elapses with the child still alive (and pattern still\n  unseen).\n* `PtyDriverEOF` — raised by `expect_prompt_ready` when the child closes\n  its PTY (typically a crash) before emitting the prompt-ready pattern.\n\nA `PtyDriverNotStarted` guard fires when any I/O method is called before\n`spawn()`. `PtyDriverError` is the shared base, so callers can `except\nPtyDriverError:` to catch the entire surface.\n\n### Default prompt-ready pattern (DEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN)\n\n```python\nDEFAULT_PROMPT_READY_PATTERN: str = r\"[>$] *\\r*\\n\"\n```\n\nMatches a bare `>` or `$` followed by optional whitespace and a CR/LF\nterminator. The pattern relies on the newline terminator rather than the\n`$` end-of-string anchor because `pexpect` compiles patterns without the\nMULTILINE flag and its incremental PTY buffer almost always has trailing\nCR/LF after the marker — an unanchored match against the newline is both\nmore permissive and more reliable. Callers that need a binary-specific\npattern (e.g. when the real `claude` REPL emits a `╭─` framed prompt)\noverride `prompt_ready_pattern` at construction.\n\n### Exit-code semantics (T02.16 AC bullet 3)\n\n`wait_exit` returns:\n\n* `int >= 0` — POSIX exit code from `child.exitstatus`.\n* `int < 0`  — POSIX-convention `-SIGNUM` when the child was killed by a\n  signal (signal code lifted from `child.signalstatus`).\n\nThe negative-on-signal contract lets callers distinguish `exit(124)`\n(timeout exit) from `SIGTERM` cleanly — important because the orchestrator\n(T03.x) maps signal termination and explicit `exit(124)` to different\noutcome statuses (`INTERRUPTED` vs `TIMEOUT`).\n\n`wait_exit` is idempotent after the child has exited: the captured exit\ncode is cached on `_exit_code` and returned on subsequent calls without\nre-driving `expect(EOF)`.\n\n### Lifecycle hygiene\n\n* `is_alive() -> bool` — `pexpect.spawn.isalive()` proxy.\n* `terminate(force: bool = False)` — SIGTERM by default, SIGKILL when\n  `force=True`. Safe to call on an already-exited child.\n* `close()` — close the PTY fd and force-close on `pexpect.ExceptionPexpect`\n  so we never leak the fd. Also pulls the exit code one last time so\n  callers that go straight to `close()` (no `wait_exit`) still get\n  `driver.exit_code` populated.\n* `__enter__` / `__exit__` — context-manager support: enters with a fresh\n  spawn, exits with `terminate()` + `close()`. Used by every interactive\n  test in `tests/cli/eval/test_pty_driver.py`.\n\n### Import strategy\n\n```python\ntry:\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.pty import pexpect as _pexpect  # vendored\nexcept ImportError:\n    import pexpect as _pexpect                                # fallback\n```\n\nThe vendored path is the long-term home (NFR-MAINT1 / T02.01). The\nfall-back keeps the module importable while T02.01's vendored sources are\nstill landing. Once T02.01 ships, the fall-back becomes dead code — this\nis intentional, not a long-term escape hatch.\n\n## Acceptance criteria mapping (T02.16)\n\n| AC bullet                                                                                                          | Evidence                                                                                                                                                  |\n|---|---|\n| `PtyDriver` in `src/superclaude/cli/eval/pty_driver.py` exposes the 5 methods.                                     | `test_method_surface_matches_comp_007_contract` — introspects `inspect.getmembers(PtyDriver, predicate=inspect.isfunction)` and asserts the five names.    |\n| A unit test spawns a real `claude --help` (or test-stub) subprocess via PTY and `expect_prompt_ready()` returns.   | `test_expect_prompt_ready_returns_before_timeout_against_stub` (always runs) + `test_real_claude_help_smoketest` (opt-in; skipped when binary absent).    |\n| `wait_exit()` captures and returns the subprocess exit code accurately.                                            | `test_wait_exit_captures_exit_code_from_stub[0,1,42,124]` parametrized + `test_wait_exit_reports_signal_termination_as_negative` + idempotency test.       |\n\nThe four extra test groups beyond the three AC bullets are defense in\ndepth — every public method has at least one positive test and one\nfailure-mode test, and the lifecycle (`spawn`/`terminate`/`close`) is\ncovered by the context-manager round-trips that wrap every interactive\ntest.\n\n## FR-G1 satisfaction\n\nThe cliEval roadmap's FR-G1 (\"real subprocess discipline\") forbids any\nin-process Anthropic SDK path under `cli/eval/`. `PtyDriver` satisfies the\nconstructive half of that requirement by being the sole entry point for\nspawning a `claude` subprocess in the harness:\n\n1. The constructor accepts `command: str | list[str]`; the cliEval\n   orchestrator passes `[\"claude\", *args]` exclusively.\n2. The spawn path is `pexpect.spawn` — a literal POSIX `fork` + `execve`\n   inside a fresh PTY. There is no Python-level shortcut that bypasses the\n   binary.\n3. Per-eval isolation is applied through `env=HomeIsolation.env()` — the\n   driver does not look at `os.environ` directly, so a forgotten env\n   override cannot leak the host's `$HOME` into the child.\n\nThe negative half (\"no SDK imports under `cli/eval/`\") is enforced by the\nban-import lint rule landed in T03.x (`COMP-013` / D-0038 family). The two\ntogether close FR-G1.\n\n## Out-of-scope (delegated downstream)\n\n* ANSI stripping + line buffering → T02.17 `PtyStream` / D-0037.\n* `claude` version pinning + capability discovery → `eval doctor`\n  subcommand and `CapabilityGates` (Phase 1).\n* SIGINT cancellation + timeout reaping → T03.x `EvalRunner` (calls\n  `terminate(force=True)` then `wait_exit`).\n\nEach of these consumes `PtyDriver` rather than reaching into pexpect\ndirectly — the driver is the single concentration point for the PTY\ncontract.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":472,"cache_read_input_tokens":155190,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":472,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":44,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"772ae136-e46c-4038-aba7-d58b744d5b50","uuid":"c1db2563-8618-4bb9-8186-bf7a890654ee"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01GgxMgjvTsZfvyqwktvT5wR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_013d3hyzWNtaFBBhdtCkJPd3","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/config/workspace/IronClaude/.dev/releases/current/cliEval/artifacts/D-0036/notes.md","content":"# D-0036 — Implementation notes (Task T02.16)\n\n## Why `default_timeout` defaults to 30 s\n\nThe roadmap surfaces NFR-PERF1 (HOME setup p50 ≤ 2.0 s at 15-eval parallel,\nT02.15) and NFR-REL1 (per-eval timeout kills + reaps, T03.x). Neither\nspecifies a hard floor for the *prompt-ready* wait — that is an eval-body\nbudget, not a driver budget. 30 s is the largest value that still feels\n\"snappy\" if a developer is staring at a doctor-style smoke test, while\nbeing generous enough that the rare cold-start (cold pip cache, fresh\nHOME, MCP gateway not yet warm) does not flake the harness. Each eval\nbody that needs a tighter or looser budget overrides via the per-call\n`timeout=` kwarg — the default is the floor, not a hard ceiling.\n\n## Why `inject_prompt` writes `\\r\\n`, not `\\n`\n\nClaude Code's REPL is line-oriented in canonical PTY mode (the `pexpect`\ndefault). The line discipline strips the `\\r` on input, so the child\nsees `\\n` either way — but writing `\\r\\n` matches what a real terminal\nemitter sends (a CR keystroke followed by the LF that the line\ndiscipline appends). Several Claude Code edge cases noted in the\nupstream ptytest fork (the original `PtySession.inject_prompt`\nimplementation) rely on this exact byte sequence. Matching the upstream\ncontract verbatim minimizes the drift surface during T02.01's vendored\nSHA reviews.\n\n## Why `read_stdout` is non-blocking by default\n\nThe cliEval orchestrator runs each eval in its own thread, and `Expect`\nprimitives (`Expect.stdout.contains`, etc.) need to assert against the\noutput stream incrementally rather than waiting for EOF. `pexpect`\nexposes `read_nonblocking` for exactly this purpose; we proxy it\ndirectly and swallow `pexpect.TIMEOUT` / `pexpect.EOF` into a clean\nempty-string return so callers can write `while not_yet_seen:\nchunk = driver.read_stdout()` loops without a try/except around every\nread.\n\nThe `timeout=0` fast-path was the obvious alternative, but\n`read_nonblocking` with a small positive timeout (say 0.2 s) is more\nforgiving across slow CI hosts. We let the caller pick by exposing\n`timeout` as a kwarg with the driver default as the fall-back.\n\n## Why `wait_exit` uses `expect(EOF, timeout=...)` instead of `child.wait()`\n\n`pexpect.spawn.wait()` does not accept a timeout parameter; it blocks\nindefinitely. The cliEval orchestrator needs to enforce per-eval\ntimeouts (NFR-REL1), so we emulate a timed wait by issuing\n`expect(EOF, timeout=T)` — which blocks until the PTY closes (i.e., the\nchild has exited) or `T` seconds elapse. The subsequent `child.wait()`\ncall is a no-op reaper at that point and populates `exitstatus` /\n`signalstatus` without blocking.\n\nThe bare `child.wait()` raises `ExceptionPexpect` when pexpect believes\nthe child is still alive; we swallow it because `expect(EOF)` already\nestablished that the child is gone — any residual race is benign and the\nnext attribute read on `child.exitstatus` returns the correct value.\n\n## Why we cache `_exit_code` on the driver\n\nTwo reasons:\n\n1. **Idempotency.** `wait_exit` is called by the orchestrator\n   (T03.x) once when the eval completes, and may be called again by the\n   reporter (T03.x) when it generates the per-eval summary. The cache\n   avoids issuing a second `expect(EOF)` against a closed PTY (which\n   would raise `EOF` again and break the contract).\n2. **Signal-termination capture.** `close()` is called by `__exit__`\n   whether or not `wait_exit` ran. If a test terminates the child via\n   `terminate(force=True)` and then exits the `with` block without\n   calling `wait_exit`, `close()` still populates `_exit_code` from\n   `child.signalstatus` so the test can read `driver.exit_code` after\n   the context manager closes.\n\n## Why we re-export the error classes via `cli/eval/__init__.py`\n\nThe cliEval orchestrator (T03.x) catches `PtyDriverError` at the top of\neach eval thread to translate driver failures into structured\n`EvalOutcome` artifacts. Routing the imports through the package's\n`__init__.py` keeps the orchestrator's import list flat and aligns with\nthe pattern already established for `HomeContainmentViolation` /\n`HookDeployFailed` / `SchemaError` (all exported the same way).\n\n## Why no test for the real `claude` REPL prompt\n\n`claude --help` is the only `claude`-invocation that is reliably\nnon-interactive across versions; the REPL prompt format has churned\nbetween releases (FR-G1 risk R1, R-013). The opt-in smoketest exercises\nthe spawn + exit-code path against the real binary without anchoring on\nany particular prompt format. Tests that need the actual REPL prompt\nwill live in the eval bodies (Phase 5), where they can be parametrized\non a `claude` version pin set by `eval doctor`.\n\n## Why we do NOT shell out to `pexpect.spawnu`\n\n`pexpect.spawn(...)` with `encoding=\"utf-8\"` is the modern equivalent of\nthe legacy `spawnu` constructor — pexpect's own docs flag `spawnu` as\ndeprecated. We stay on the canonical entry point so future pexpect\nupgrades (NFR-MAINT1 `pexpect>=4.9` floor) do not surface deprecation\nwarnings inside the harness.\n\n## Known limitations\n\n* **Cooked-mode line discipline.** `write_stdin(\"abcde\")` writes the\n  five characters to the PTY but the child's `sys.stdin.readline()` will\n  not return until a line terminator arrives. The test\n  `test_write_stdin_does_not_append_newline` documents this by writing\n  the terminator in a separate call and asserting on the absence of any\n  `\"got=\"` output in the interim. Callers that need raw input must\n  switch the PTY to raw mode themselves (out of scope for v1).\n* **Negative exit codes only on signal.** A child that exits with a\n  *signal* gets `-SIGNUM`; a child that exits with a *negative status\n  set via `_exit(-1)`* is impossible at the POSIX level (the kernel\n  masks the status to 8 bits unsigned), so the negative-on-signal\n  contract is unambiguous.\n* **No streaming `read_stdout` cursor.** Each `read_stdout` call drains\n  whatever pexpect has buffered; callers that need a single\n  monotonically-growing transcript build it by concatenating chunks\n  themselves (the round-trip test does exactly this). The\n  `PtyStream` ANSI/buffer layer (T02.17) wraps this into a proper\n  line-iterator with a `PtyTimeout`-aware deadline.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":3557,"cache_read_input_tokens":155662,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":3557,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":46,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"772ae136-e46c-4038-aba7-d58b744d5b50","uuid":"603f529c-b75b-472f-8782-75a884881627"}

We therefore split\n    the write into two parts: ``write_stdin(\"abcde\")`` (no newline) followed\n    by ``write_stdin(\"\\\\r\")`` (terminator only). If ``write_stdin`` were\n    secretly appending a newline, the stub's ``readline`` would unblock on\n    the first call and return ``'abcde\\\\n'`` -- so the absence of any output\n    after the first call AND the presence of ``\"got='abcde'\"`` after the\n    second call together pin the contract.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    source = textwrap.dedent(\n        \"\"\"\n        import sys\n        sys.stdout.write(\"> \\\\r\\\\n\"); sys.stdout.flush()\n        line = sys.stdin.readline()\n        sys.stdout.write(\"got=\" + repr(line.rstrip()) + \"\\\\r\\\\n\")\n        sys.stdout.flush()\n        \"\"\"\n    ).strip()\n    with _make_driver(source) as driver:\n        driver.expect_prompt_ready()\n        wrote = driver.write_stdin(\"abcde\")\n        assert wrote >= 5\n        # No newline was sent -- the child must still be blocked on readline.\n        idle = driver.read_stdout(size=64, timeout=0.3)\n        assert \"got=\" not in idle, (\n            f\"write_stdin appears to have auto-appended a newline: idle={idle!r}\"\n        )\n        driver.write_stdin(\"\\r\")\n        collected = \"\"\n        deadline = time.perf_counter() + 5.0\n        while time.perf_counter() < deadline:\n            collected += driver.read_stdout(timeout=0.5)\n            if \"got='abcde'\" in collected:\n                break\n        assert \"got='abcde'\" in collected, (\n            f\"expected stub to echo got='abcde' after newline; got: {collected!r}\"\n        )","replace_all":false}}],"terminal_reason":"completed","fast_mode_state":"off","uuid":"b45c751c-be2a-41a3-b6b0-a20ed7e2e7c4"}
